# LLM Manager — VM Reproducibility + GitHub CI/CD Production Deployment Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-25  
**Repository:** `startupteams/llm-manager-project-framework`  
**Current production:** VM114 on MARION-IA-USA / MIAM-00135

## 0. Goal

Build a safe path from reviewed GitHub source to production:

```text
branch/PR
  -> GitHub CI
  -> merge to main
  -> immutable release artifact
  -> automatic staging deployment
  -> staging acceptance
  -> human-approved production promotion
  -> VM114
  -> health checks
  -> automatic application rollback on failure
```

The first gate is **reproducibility**:

> A clean Ubuntu 24.04 VM must be deployable from this repository plus documented external dependencies/secrets and behave like the current VM114.

If that fails, fix the source capture or deployment method before enabling production CD.

## 1. Current-state findings

The repo already captures:

```text
service/app/
service/collectors/
service/control/
ops/systemd/
ops/nginx/
ops/litellm/
config/examples/
db/schema/schema.sql
```

Current deployment maps these to `/opt/llm-manager`, `/etc/systemd/system`, `/etc/nginx`, and `/etc/llm-manager`.

Current production includes FastAPI/uvicorn, LiteLLM, recovery, GPU-power collector, Emporia collector, nginx, PostgreSQL, LLDAP, Proxmox control, model endpoints, and a documented `/agents/` dependency.

Important gaps before CI/CD:

1. No `.github/workflows/` exists.
2. No automated clean-VM installer/bootstrap exists.
3. Systemd units hard-code `/opt/llm-manager/...`.
4. DB changes are manual/additive; no migration runner exists.
5. `litellm_config.yaml` is generated runtime state and must not blindly overwrite production.
6. Current tests are too small to prove hostability.
7. No real staging VM exists.
8. Resolve the documented Python-version inconsistency; the captured runtime file says Python 3.12.3.
9. Determine whether AgentManager on `127.0.0.1:8200` is an external dependency or missing captured code.
10. Recovery/registry synchronization can mutate state and must be handled deliberately during deployment.

## 2. Recommended production layout

Do not deploy with `git pull` directly into the live tree.

Use immutable releases:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
├── releases/
│   ├── <git-sha-1>/
│   └── <git-sha-2>/
├── current -> /opt/llm-manager/releases/<active-sha>
└── shared/
```

Keep mutable/secrets outside releases:

```text
/etc/llm-manager/
├── secrets/
├── tls/
├── litellm.env
└── litellm_config.yaml
```

Update systemd to point to `/opt/llm-manager/current/...`.

Benefits: exact Git-SHA identity, atomic-ish promotion, simple rollback, no untracked production edits.

## 3. Repo additions

Add:

```text
.github/workflows/
  ci.yml
  deploy-staging.yml
  deploy-production.yml

deploy/
  README.md
  build-release.sh
  bootstrap-vm.sh
  preflight.sh
  deploy-release.sh
  healthcheck.sh
  rollback.sh
  verify-parity.sh

db/migrations/
  README.md
  <numbered migrations>.sql

tests/
  unit/
  integration/
  fixtures/
  mocks/

docs/
  CI_CD.md
  DEPLOYMENT.md
```

## 4. Phase 1 — production completeness audit

Before production automation, compare GitHub with VM114.

Capture from VM114:

```text
OS/kernel
Python version
relevant apt packages
venv freeze
LLM Manager-related systemd units
nginx configuration
LiteLLM command/config behavior
source files
collectors/control code
required directories
ownership/permissions
required secret filenames
TLS filenames
DB host/schema
LLDAP
external dependencies
AgentManager
ports
timers/jobs
```

Never capture secret values.

Create a non-secret machine-readable deployment manifest such as:

```text
deploy/production-manifest.yaml
```

Produce a completeness table:

| Production item | In repo? | Reproducible? | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| FastAPI app | | | |
| Recovery | | | |
| Collectors | | | |
| PVE control | | | |
| LiteLLM | | | |
| nginx | | | |
| DB schema/migrations | | | |
| TLS | external | | |
| LLDAP | external | | |
| AgentManager | determine | determine | |

The repo is deployable only when every live component is reproducible from Git or explicitly documented as an external dependency.

## 5. Phase 2 — fresh-VM bootstrap

Create an idempotent `deploy/bootstrap-vm.sh` or equivalent.

A supported fresh Ubuntu 24.04 VM must be installable from a release artifact.

Bootstrap must install/configure:

```text
required OS packages
Python 3.12.x
release-specific venv
pinned dependencies
nginx
PostgreSQL client
directories
systemd units
LiteLLM
application source
collectors
control shim
permissions
```

It must not embed secrets.

Document the exact required secret-file contract from the live system. GitHub holds no production application secret values.

## 6. Phase 3 — dedicated staging VM

Create a MARION staging VM.

Use:

```text
Ubuntu 24.04
same Python minor as production
same systemd/nginx structure
separate IP/DNS
separate staging DB and role
non-production credentials
```

Integration rules:

- PostgreSQL: use `llmmanager_staging` or equivalent, never production tables.
- LLDAP: dedicated test account or test LDAP.
- Proxmox: mock in CI; staging gets read-only/restricted access only if needed.
- Inference: use mock OpenAI-compatible endpoints for most tests.
- Emporia/power: mock in CI; real integration only as an explicit read-only smoke.

## 7. Phase 4 — clean-room hostability gate

Provision staging **only** from:

```text
GitHub artifact
+ documented external secrets
+ documented external endpoints
```

Do not copy unknown files from VM114.

Pass criteria:

```text
systemd units install/start
nginx validates
/healthz healthy
/login/admin path behaves
/v1/models works
LiteLLM starts
registry sync works against staging data
history API works
benchmark API works
collectors start in safe mode
database initializes/migrates
```

Then reboot staging and repeat.

Any dependency on an undocumented manual file/edit means the gate fails and source/deployment automation must be fixed.

## 8. Phase 5 — database migrations

Keep `db/schema/schema.sql` as a clean-install reference, not an in-place migration tool.

Add numbered migrations and a ledger:

```text
schema_migrations
- version
- checksum
- applied_at
- git_sha
```

Deployment:

1. inspect applied versions;
2. verify checksums;
3. apply pending migrations in order;
4. record completion.

Prefer backward-compatible expand/contract changes.

Destructive migrations must not auto-deploy; require a maintenance plan, approval, backup, and explicit recovery path.

Before production migrations, make a protected DB backup locally. Never upload production DB dumps to GitHub artifacts.

## 9. Phase 6 — CI

Create `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

Triggers:

```yaml
pull_request:
push:
  branches: [main]
```

Use GitHub-hosted runners for PR CI. Do not run arbitrary PR code on MARION self-hosted deployment runners.

Required jobs:

### Syntax / existing tests

```bash
python3 -m py_compile service/app/*.py service/collectors/*.py service/control/*.py
python3 service/app/v011_cost_tests.py
```

### Clean dependency install

Use the authoritative Python version and locked dependencies in a clean venv. Fail if install is not reproducible.

### Secret scan

Use `gitleaks` or equivalent and fail on likely committed credentials.

### Dependency audit

Use `pip-audit`. Decide which severity blocks merge so inherited debt does not make CI permanently unusable.

### Config validation

Validate YAML/LiteLLM structure, systemd units, nginx syntax, and Python imports/config.

For nginx CI, use temporary certificates/config substitution rather than production TLS files.

### DB validation

Launch temporary PostgreSQL, apply schema + migrations + sanitized fixtures, then exercise expected DB queries.

### Integration smoke

Run FastAPI with mock dependencies and test:

```text
/healthz
/v1/models
selected read APIs
deployment history
benchmark history
maintenance/recovery logic
```

Add regression tests for:

```text
desired_service_state
desired_power_state
STOPPED_INTENTIONAL
```

### Release artifact

After required CI passes, build:

```text
llm-manager-<git-sha>.tar.gz
```

Include source, ops, deploy scripts, DB files, config, release manifest, Git SHA/checksums.

Exclude secrets, logs, DB contents, venvs, caches.

## 10. Phase 7 — deployment runner

Use a dedicated internal self-hosted GitHub Actions runner for deployment jobs only.

Prefer a small MARION VM/LXC, not VM114.

Rules:

- scoped to this repo;
- no PR jobs;
- network access to staging and VM114;
- no app/database secrets;
- dedicated SSH deploy key;
- no GPU/model-server role.

On VM114 create a restricted `llm-manager-deploy` account, preferably with sudo only to a reviewed root-owned deployment script rather than unrestricted root SSH.

## 11. Phase 8 — staging CD

Create `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`.

Trigger on successful CI for `main`.

Sequence:

1. download exact CI artifact;
2. verify checksum;
3. transfer to staging;
4. run preflight;
5. install `/opt/llm-manager/releases/<sha>`;
6. build release-specific `.venv`;
7. apply staging migrations;
8. validate systemd/nginx;
9. update `current` symlink;
10. restart staging services;
11. run healthcheck/acceptance;
12. record deployed SHA.

Staging deployment should be automatic after merge.

## 12. Phase 9 — production CD

Create `.github/workflows/deploy-production.yml`.

Initially trigger via:

```text
workflow_dispatch
or protected-environment approval after staging success
```

Use a GitHub Environment named `production` with a required human reviewer.

Production must deploy the **same artifact/SHA that passed staging**. Do not rebuild it.

## 13. Production deployment sequence

### Preflight

Verify:

```text
artifact/checksum
current release
disk space
runtime compatibility
external files
DB
LLDAP
nginx
systemd
current /healthz
```

Snapshot:

```text
active SHA
systemd state
/v1/models
aliases/routes
health
```

Create pre-migration DB backup if needed.

### Suppress interference

Stop `llm-manager-recovery.service` first.

Do not stop hosted model servers just because the manager is being upgraded.

### Install candidate

Install candidate release and `.venv` under `releases/<sha>`.

Do not alter previous releases.

Apply only pending safe migrations.

### Candidate validation

Before moving `current`, run syntax/tests/config/migration checks inside the candidate release.

### Activate

Move `current` to candidate, run `systemctl daemon-reload`, then restart only affected components.

Typical order:

```text
llm-manager-web
llm-manager-collector
llm-manager-emporia
litellm if required
nginx reload only if changed
llm-manager-recovery LAST
```

Regenerate LiteLLM routing with the release's `litellm_sync.py`; do not overwrite production with a stale captured `litellm_config.yaml`.

### Post-deploy smoke

Require:

```text
all relevant services active
/healthz healthy
/v1/models correct
routes present
history API healthy
benchmark API healthy
TLS/nginx healthy
LiteLLM healthy
one approved local-model completion
one approved cloud completion if available
no restart/recovery storm
```

Watch journals briefly before marking success.

## 14. Automatic application rollback

If smoke tests fail:

1. stop recovery;
2. point `current` back to previous release;
3. daemon-reload;
4. restart previous app/services;
5. restore/regenerate routing as needed;
6. healthcheck;
7. restart recovery last;
8. mark deploy failed.

Retain at least five known-good release directories initially.

Automatic rollback is only safe with backward-compatible DB changes, so destructive migrations must be blocked from ordinary CD.

## 15. Generated LiteLLM config policy

Treat the captured `ops/litellm/litellm_config.yaml` as reference/capture, not something CD blindly overwrites.

Production routing should be regenerated from LLM Manager state with `litellm_sync.py`.

CI/CD should compare route/model counts before/after and alert on unexpected alias/model loss.

## 16. AgentManager completeness gate

The architecture documents:

```text
/agents/ -> 127.0.0.1:8200
```

Decide:

### If external
Document AgentManager as an external dependency with endpoint/health contract.

### If part of LLM Manager
Capture its source and service definition and include it in bootstrap, CI, staging, deployment, and smoke tests.

Do not claim complete VM reproducibility while relying on uncaptured code.

## 17. Branch protection

Configure `main`:

- no direct pushes;
- PR review required;
- required CI checks;
- current branch requirement where practical.

Use Git SHA as release identity.

Add active build metadata to `/healthz` or a status endpoint:

```json
{
  "ok": true,
  "version": "...",
  "git_sha": "...",
  "build_time": "...",
  "schema_version": "..."
}
```

## 18. Definition of done

- [ ] VM114-to-repo completeness audit complete.
- [ ] Every live component is captured or explicitly external.
- [ ] Python runtime version discrepancy resolved.
- [ ] Fresh Ubuntu staging VM deploys entirely from Git artifact + documented external inputs.
- [ ] Staging survives reboot.
- [ ] `/healthz`, `/v1/models`, history, benchmark, LiteLLM, nginx pass.
- [ ] CI runs on PRs.
- [ ] CI includes syntax/tests, secret scan, config validation, DB test.
- [ ] Immutable artifact is built and identified by SHA.
- [ ] Merge to main auto-deploys the same artifact to staging.
- [ ] Production requires protected/manual approval initially.
- [ ] Production does not use `git pull`.
- [ ] Production secrets never enter GitHub.
- [ ] Release-directory + `current` symlink deploy works.
- [ ] Previous application release rollback is demonstrated.
- [ ] Migration policy prevents unsafe auto rollback.
- [ ] Recovery starts last.
- [ ] Running Git SHA is observable.
- [ ] `docs/CI_CD.md` and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` are complete.

## 19. AI-agent implementation order

```text
1. Create issue + branch.
2. Read AGENTS/requirements/architecture.
3. Audit repo vs VM114.
4. Resolve missing components/external dependencies.
5. Resolve Python version.
6. Add manifest/bootstrap/preflight/health/rollback scripts.
7. Build clean staging VM.
8. Make clean-room install pass.
9. Add migration mechanism.
10. Add CI/tests.
11. Add immutable release packaging.
12. Add automatic staging deployment.
13. Convert production to releases/current in controlled maintenance.
14. Prove manual artifact deploy.
15. Prove rollback.
16. Add protected production workflow.
17. Update docs/requirements/status/ADR/TDR as needed.
18. Handoff exact branch, commits, PR, staging evidence, production evidence, rollback evidence.
```

## 20. Initial production policy

Use:

```text
PR -> CI -> merge -> auto staging -> human approval -> production
```

Do **not** start with:

```text
merge -> immediate production
```

After several successful releases and proven rollbacks, the team can decide whether automatic production promotion is appropriate.

The desired end state is a production LLM Manager that is fully Git-derived, reproducible on a VM, observable by Git SHA, and safely deployable/rollbackable without manual source editing on VM114.

# 21. Critical implementation update — MIAM-00111 active VM changed from VM103 to VM102

The live LLM Manager dashboard is still probing the historical VM103 endpoint:

```text
MIAM-00111 / VM103
10.0.20.161:8000
```

The current active inference deployment is:

```text
MIAM-00111 / VM102
10.0.20.168:8000
qwen3.8-flash-next
```

This must be corrected as part of the CI/CD implementation work.

## 21.1 Immediate source corrections

### `service/app/main.py`

Replace the stale active host entry:

```python
{"name": "MIAM-00111 / VM103", "ip": "10.0.20.161"}
```

with:

```python
{"name": "MIAM-00111 / VM102", "ip": "10.0.20.168"}
```

The current `VLLM_HOSTS` list is used by `/api/status`, deployment-control APIs, logs, and hosting-command-preset APIs.

### `service/app/v011_core.py`

Replace the active Proxmox/recovery mapping:

```python
"10.0.20.161": ("miam00111", 103)
```

with:

```python
"10.0.20.168": ("miam00111", 102)
```

This affects VM status, VM power actions, recovery, registry sync, and desired-state enforcement.

## 21.2 PostgreSQL active-host correction

The active `hosts` record for MIAM-00111 must represent VM102:

```text
name: MIAM-00111 / VM102
guest_ip: 10.0.20.168
node: miam00111
vmid: 102
desired_power_state: RUNNING
desired_service_state: SERVING
management_mode: MANAGED
```

VM103 must remain preserved as a rollback deployment, but must not remain the active host target.

## 21.3 Registry refresh bug

`sync_registry()` currently refreshes health/context/routability but can leave stale endpoint metadata.

Update its `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` behavior so active records refresh at least:

```text
engine
backend_url
context_limit
health
routable
last_verified
updated_at
```

After the switch, no active `model_registry.backend_url` for MIAM-00111 should still point to:

```text
http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1
```

The active Flash-Next backend should be:

```text
http://10.0.20.168:8000/v1
```

## 21.4 Historical deployment rule

Do not rewrite history.

Historical deployment revisions for VM103/.161/Qwen3.8-27B remain valid historical records.

Create/retain a separate active revision for:

```text
VM102
10.0.20.168
Qwen3.8-Flash-Next
TP2 / PP3 / EP
70K context
```

The active deployment changes; historical evidence does not.

## 21.5 Hosting-command preset handling

`hosting_command_presets.scope_key` currently uses IP address for single-host presets.

Before changing `.161 -> .168`:

1. inspect presets scoped to `10.0.20.161`;
2. preserve VM103/Qwen3.8-27B rollback presets as historical rollback material;
3. copy only reusable presets that actually apply to VM102;
4. avoid silently rebinding historical presets to a different VM/model.

Future improvement:

> Presets should be associated with stable deployment/host IDs rather than mutable IP addresses.

## 21.6 Required validation after immediate correction

Run:

```text
1. Restart llm-manager-web.
2. Restart llm-manager-recovery.
3. Run registry sync.
4. Verify /api/status shows MIAM-00111 / VM102.
5. Verify health probes target 10.0.20.168:8000.
6. Verify /v1/models exposes qwen3.8-flash-next when healthy.
7. Verify VM power/recovery actions resolve to VMID 102.
8. Verify VM103 remains intentionally stopped and is not auto-started.
9. Verify code alias resolves to qwen3.8-flash-next.
10. Verify no active registry route still points to .161.
```

## 21.7 Architectural fix — one source of truth for active placement

The hard-coded correction above is only the immediate fix.

The CI/CD/refactor sprint must remove active deployment placement from hard-coded Python constants.

Target data model:

```text
physical host
   -> active deployment
   -> active VM
   -> guest IP
   -> actual model/runtime
```

The following consumers must all derive the active target from the same database-backed source of truth:

```text
dashboard/status API
health probes
recovery
VM power control
registry sync
deployment-control APIs
hosting-command presets
routing
```

Desired future behavior:

```text
Promote VM102
  -> update active deployment in DB
  -> dashboard changes automatically
  -> probes change automatically
  -> recovery targets VM102 automatically
  -> registry backend_url changes automatically
  -> no Python source edit
```

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/llm-manager-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

{"output": "# LLM Manager Architecture\n\nThis document uses the 12-section arc42 structure with C4-inspired Mermaid views.\n\n## 1. Introduction and Goals\n\nLLM Manager is the Startup Teams infrastructure service responsible for AI-agent runtime/inference infrastructure. Its primary goals are:\n\n1. abstract heterogeneous local/cloud inference behind a stable management boundary;\n2. preserve reproducible model/runtime configurations and rollback;\n3. expose health, capacity, benchmark, usage, and eventually cost information;\n4. allow ACMS to orchestrate agents without managing low-level inference infrastructure;\n5. make local model experimentation safer and more measurable.\n\nQuality priorities are operational safety, reproducibility, observability, agent capacity, model quality, performance, and cost transparency.\n\n## 2. Architecture Constraints\n\n- Initial deployment is MARION-IA-USA but protocols should not be Marion-specific.\n- Proxmox hosts/VMs are part of the current execution environment.\n- Local inference includes vLLM and llama.cpp; external inference is routed through provider integrations.\n- Some high-value models require patched/version-pinned runtimes.\n- Known-good runtimes must not be upgraded in place during experiments.\n- Human review is required before merge and for significant architecture/policy decisions.\n- ACMS project/context/work responsibilities stay outside LLM Manager.\n- PDU Manager remains authoritative for raw electrical measurements.\n\n## 3. Context and Scope\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    U[Human operators] --> L[LLM Manager]\n    A[ACMS] -->|approved constraints, runtime/model requests| L\n    L -->|status, usage, cost, capacity| A\n    L --> H[Agent runtimes: Hermes / Codex / Pi / future]\n    L --> P[Proxmox + local model hosts]\n    L --> C[Cloud inference providers]\n    D[PDU Manager] -->|power / energy measurements| L\n```\n\n### In scope\n\n- runtime registration/metadata;\n- model/inference registry and routing;\n- local model server lifecycle coordination;\n- cloud/provider model routing metadata;\n- health/capacity;\n- deployment revisions and rollback metadata;\n- benchmarking;\n- raw usage and inference-cost calculation/reporting;\n...", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "# Implementation Status and Known Limitations\n\n**Snapshot basis:** September 2026 handoffs, design discussions, and the 2026-09-25 code capture of the deployed service on VM114 (`service/`, `ops/`, `db/schema/` in this repo). This file is descriptive, not the live source of truth. Verify the running system before operating it.\n\n## Implemented / observed in the current deployment\n\n### Deployed components (from captured systemd units)\n\n| Unit | Process | Bind |\n|---|---|---|\n| `llm-manager-web.service` | `uvicorn main:app --workers 2` (FastAPI web shell + API) | 127.0.0.1:8001 |\n| `litellm.service` | LiteLLM proxy gateway (`/etc/llm-manager/litellm_config.yaml`, auto-generated) | 127.0.0.1:4000 |\n| AgentManager (served under `/agents/`) | agent provisioning/chat UI on :8200 | 127.0.0.1:8200 |\n| `llm-manager-recovery.service` | `v011_recovery.py` — desired-state engine + `sync_registry` tick | — |\n| `llm-manager-collector.service` | `gpu_power_collector.py` (GPU_SAMPLE_SECONDS=20) | — |\n| `llm-manager-emporia.service` | `emporia_adapter.py --loop` (facility power) | — |\n\nnginx fronts everything: `:80 /healthz` plain + redirect, `:443` TLS with routes to web (8001), LiteLLM `/v1`+`/ui`+`/key/` (4000), and `/agents/` (8200).\n\n### Core service and routing\n\n- LLM Manager is deployed on VM114 in MARION-IA-USA.\n- Service health is exposed through `/healthz` (on the web shell, port 8001, proxied by nginx).\n- OpenAI-compatible model inventory/routing is exposed through `/v1/models` via the manager/routing layer.\n- Local vLLM endpoints, llama.cpp endpoints, and cloud/provider-backed routes have all been managed through the service.\n- A recovery/control subsystem (`v011_recovery.py`) tracks desired state and can attempt service/VM recovery; it also runs `sync_registry()` which owns model-registry rows and the `fast`/`code`/`frontier` alias repointing.\n- Proxmox control is an allowlisted shim (`service/control/pve_ops.py`): only vm-status/vm-start/vm-shutdown/vm-reboot against a fixed IP→(node,vmid) map; no force-stop, no arbitrary VMID.\n- Authentication is via LLDAP (LDAP on port 3890) for the `/admin` web shell.\n\n### Deployment history and benchmarks\n\nThe September 2026 deployment added:\n\n- `deployment_revisions` history;\n- expanded `benchmark_runs` fields linked to deployment revisions;\n- history/benchmark API endpoints;\n- dashboard deployment-history display;\n- successful-versus-failed visibility filtering;\n- baseline hosting-command presets;\n- non-destructive `Load Configuration`/pending-configuration behavior.\n\n### Model-serving examples\n\nRecent MARION work has demonstrated:\n\n- Qwen3.8-27B on RTX 3080 hardware at 70K context using a custom vLLM fork.\n- Qwen3.6-35B-A3B using DP/EP and, on some hosts, a custom TURBOQUANT path.\n- Gemma 4 26B-A4B as a two-node local pool with strong C4/C8 throughput and Hermes tool-call success.\n- Qwen3.8-Flash-Next successfully qualified on six RTX 3080 20 GB GPUs using a pinned PP-enabled Ampere vLLM stack, TP2×PP3+EP, FP8 PLE CPU offload, 70K context, and working vision.\n- llama.cpp on MIAM-00149, with larger 27B CPU-only experiments shown to be impractically slow for the desired 20 tok/s target.\n\nThese examples are operational evidence, not permanent product requirements.\n\n## Partially implemented / not yet proven complete\n\n- End-to-end `Restore & Serve` automation has been designed but should be treated as incomplete until the full confirmed activation + automatic rollback workflow is tested.\n- Sticky-session routing and explicit per-node concurrency caps are not part of the older v0.11 baseline.\n- Runtime capability discovery across Hermes/Codex/Pi is not yet a stable generic interface.\n- ACMS-facing management API/authentication contract is not approved.\n- Configuration locking/frozen versions are not yet defined as a complete feature.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Never loops forever (§B4).\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport traceback\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/opt/llm-manager/app\")\nfrom v011_core import (NODE_MAP, pg, vm_status, vm_power, probe_host,  # noqa: E402\n                       sync_registry, HOST_IPS)\n\nVM_START_MAX = 2\nSERVICE_RESTART_MAX = 2\nSERVICE_RESTART_COOLDOWN_S = 15 * 60\nTICK_SECONDS = 60\n\n_audit_file = \"/var/log/llm-manager/recovery-engine.log\"\n\n\ndef audit(ip, event_type, detail):\n    line = {\"ts\": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(), \"ip\": ip,\n            \"event_type\": event_type, \"detail\": detail}\n    try:\n        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(_audit_file), exist_ok=True)\n        with open(_audit_file, \"a\") as f:\n            f.write(json.dumps(line) + \"\\n\")\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    conn = pg()\n    if conn:\n        try:\n            with conn.cursor() as cur:\n                cur.execute(\"\"\"INSERT INTO recovery_events (ip, event_type, detail)\n                               VALUES (%s,%s,%s)\"\"\", (ip, event_type, json.dumps(detail)))\n            conn.commit()\n        except Exception:\n            conn.rollback()\n        finally:\n            conn.close()\n\n\ndef recent_count(ip, event_type, minutes=30):\n    conn = pg()\n    if not conn:\n        return 99  # fail-safe: no DB -> no recovery actions\n    try:\n        with conn.cursor() as cur:\n            cur.execute(\"\"\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM recovery_events\n                           WHERE ip=%s AND event_type=%s\n                             AND created_at > now() - (%s || ' minutes')::interval\"\"\",\n                        (ip, event_type, str(minutes)))\n            return cur.fetchone()[0]\n    finally:\n        conn.close()\n\n\ndef recover_vm_start(ip, desired):\n    \"\"\"Bounded VM start when unexpectedly stopped (§B2).\"\"\"\n    if recent_count(ip, \"recovery_vm_start\") >= VM_START_MAX:\n        audit(ip, \"recovery_failed\", {\"reason\": \"vm start attempts exhausted\",\n                                      \"desired\": desired})\n        return\n    audit(ip, \"recovery_vm_start\", {\"reason\": \"unexpected stop\", \"attempt\": recent_count(ip, \"recovery_vm_start\") + 1})\n    vm_power(ip, \"start\")\n    # wait for boot + service\n    for _ in range(40):\n        time.sleep(15)\n        st = vm_status(ip).get(\"status\")\n        if st == \"running\":", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Never prints secrets.\"\"\"\nimport json, ssl, sys, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n\nPVE = 'https://10.0.20.100:8006'\nTICKET_FILE = '/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/.pve_ticket'\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context(); CTX.check_hostname = False; CTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\n\n\ndef _login():\n    creds = open('/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/creds/pve_root.txt').read().split()\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({'username': f'{creds[0]}@pam', 'password': creds[1]}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + '/api2/json/access/ticket', data=data, method='POST')\n    r = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=15))['data']\n    with open(TICKET_FILE, 'w') as f:\n        f.write(r['ticket'] + '\\n' + r['CSRFPreventionToken'])\n    os.chmod(TICKET_FILE, 0o600)\n\n\ndef _tickets():\n    try:\n        lines = open(TICKET_FILE).read().split('\\n')\n        return lines[0], lines[1]\n    except Exception:\n        _login()\n        return _tickets()\n\n\ndef api(method, path, body=None, retry=True):\n    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}'}\n    data = None\n    if body is not None:\n        enc = urllib.parse.urlencode(body).encode()\n        if method == 'GET':\n            # GET params must go in the querystring for PVE\n            path = path + ('&' if '?' in path else '?') + enc.decode()\n        else:\n            data = enc\n    if method != 'GET':\n        headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = csrf\n    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n    try:\n        resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=180)\n        return json.load(resp)\n    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n        if e.code == 401 and retry:\n            _login()\n            return api(method, path, body, retry=False)\n        raise\n\n\ndef find_vm(node_hint=None, vmid=None, ip=None):\n    \"\"\"Locate a VM/LXC by vmid or current IP (via guest agent if possible).\"\"\"\n    vms = []\n    nodes = [n['node'] for n in api('GET', '/api2/json/nodes')['data']]\n    for node in nodes:\n        for vm in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu')['data']:\n            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': vm['vmid'], 'name': vm.get('name', ''), 'status': vm['status'], 'type': 'qemu'})\n        for ct in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc')['data']:\n            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': ct['vmid'], 'name': ct.get('name', ''), 'status': ct['status'], 'type': 'lxc'})\n    return vms\n\n\ndef guest_exec(node, vmid, command, timeout=120):\n    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\n    PVE expects 'command' as repeated form fields (arg encode_array).\"\"\"\n    # PVE array convention: repeat the parameter name with each value\n    # NOTE: 'timeout' is NOT an accepted param for agent/exec (400s); the exec returns a pid\n    parts = []\n    for c in command:\n        parts.append('command=' + urllib.parse.quote(c, safe=''))\n    body = '&'.join(parts).encode()\n    url = PVE + f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec'\n    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}', 'CSRFPreventionToken': csrf,\n               'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded'}\n    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=body, headers=headers, method='POST')\n    resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=timeout + 30)\n    pdata = json.load(resp)['data']", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "pdata = json.load(resp)['data']\n    pid = pdata['pid'] if isinstance(pdata, dict) else pdata\n    import time\n    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n    while time.time() < deadline:\n        time.sleep(2)\n        try:\n            st = api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec-status', {'pid': pid})['data']\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n        if st.get('exited'):\n            return st.get('out-data', ''), st.get('err-data', ''), st.get('exitcode', -1)\n    return '', 'TIMEOUT', -1\n\n\ndef lxc_exec(node, vmid, command):\n    r = api('POST', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc/{vmid}/exec', {'command': json.dumps(command)})\n    # LXC exec via API returns after completion\n    return r\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    cmd = sys.argv[1]\n    if cmd == 'find':\n        for v in find_vm():\n            print(f\"{v['vmid']:>5} {v['type']:<4} {v['status']:<7} {v['node']:<12} {v['name']}\")\n    elif cmd == 'exec':\n        node, vmid, command = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4]\n        out, err, code = guest_exec(node, vmid, ['bash', '-lc', command])\n        print(out)\n        if err: print('STDERR:', err, file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(0 if code == 0 else code)\n    elif cmd == 'api':\n        method, path = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n        body = json.loads(sys.argv[4]) if len(sys.argv) > 4 else None\n        print(json.dumps(api(method, path, body), indent=2)[:8000])", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"content": "1|name: deploy-production\n2|\n3|# §12: production CD — workflow_dispatch ONLY initially. GitHub Environment\n4|# `production` requires a human reviewer. Deploys the SAME artifact/SHA that\n5|# passed staging — the runner downloads the artifact, never rebuilds.\n6|\n7|on:\n8|  workflow_dispatch:\n9|    inputs:\n10|      git_sha:\n11|        description: \"Release git SHA to promote (must exist as an artifact)\"\n12|        required: true\n13|        type: string\n14|\n15|permissions:\n16|  contents: read\n17|  actions: read\n18|\n19|concurrency:\n20|  group: production-deploy\n21|  cancel-in-progress: false\n22|\n23|jobs:\n24|  deploy-production:\n25|    environment: production\n26|    runs-on: [self-hosted, marion, llm-manager-deploy]\n27|    steps:\n28|      - name: Locate artifact for requested SHA\n29|        uses: actions/github-script@v7\n30|        with:\n31|          script: |\n32|            const sha = context.payload.inputs.git_sha;\n33|            const runs = await github.paginate(github.rest.actions.listWorkflowRuns, {\n34|              owner: context.repo.owner, repo: context.repo.repo,\n35|              workflow_id: \"ci.yml\", status: \"success\", head_branch: \"main\",\n36|              per_page: 100,\n37|            });\n38|            for (const run of runs) {\n39|              if (run.head_sha.startsWith(sha)) {\n40|                const arts = await github.rest.actions.listWorkflowRunArtifacts({\n41|                  owner: context.repo.owner, repo: context.repo.repo, run_id: run.id,\n42|                });\n43|                const art = arts.data.artifacts.find(a => a.name.startsWith(\"llm-manager-\"));\n44|                if (art) {\n45|                  const dl = await github.rest.actions.downloadArtifact({\n46|                    owner: context.repo.owner, repo: context.repo.repo,\n47|                    artifact_id: art.id, archive_format: \"zip\",\n48|                  });\n49|                  const fs = require(\"fs\");\n50|                  fs.writeFileSync(`${process.env.RUNNER_TEMP}/artifact.zip`, Buffer.from(dl.data));\n51|                  console.log(`using artifact from run ${run.id} (${run.head_sha})`);\n52|                  return;\n53|                }\n54|              }\n55|            }\n56|            throw new Error(`no successful CI artifact found for sha ${sha}`);\n57|\n58|      - name: Verify checksum\n59|        run: |\n60|          cd \"$RUNNER_TEMP\"\n61|          unzip -o artifact.zip\n62|          ART=$(find . -maxdepth 2 -name 'llm-manager-*.tar.gz' | head -1)\n63|          sha256sum -c \"${ART}.sha256\"\n64|          MAN_SHA=$(tar -xzOf \"$ART\" --wildcards '*/release_manifest.json' | python3 -c 'import json,sys;print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\"git_sha\"])')\n65|          echo \"artifact git_sha: $MAN_SHA\"\n66|          case \"$MAN_SHA\" in \"${{ inputs.git_sha }}\"*) ;; *) echo \"SHA MISMATCH\"; exit 1;; esac\n67|          echo \"ARTIFACT=$ART\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n68|\n69|      - name: Transfer to VM114\n70|        run: scp \"$ARTIFACT\" \"$ARTIFACT.sha256\" llm-manager-deploy@10.0.20.108:/tmp/\n71|\n72|      - name: Production release transaction (preflight → install → validate →\n73|          migrate → activate → recovery-last restart → smoke → auto-rollback)\n74|        run: |\n75|          ssh llm-manager-deploy@10.0.20.108 \"bash -s\" <<'REMOTE'\n76|          set -euo pipefail\n77|          ART=$(ls /tmp/llm-manager-*.tar.gz | head -1)\n78|          sudo /opt/llm-manager-deploy/deploy-wrapper.sh \"$ART\"\n79|          REMOTE\n80|\n81|      - name: Post-deploy acceptance\n82|        run: ssh llm-manager-deploy@10.0.20.108 \"sudo /opt/llm-manager-deploy/healthcheck-wrapper.sh\"\n83|", "total_lines": 82, "file_size": 3361, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}
